LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies
The testimonies provided by Lang suggest that Shaun was an accomplice in hiding the president's body, and that the body was hidden inside a monster costume. However, the evidence does not explicitly support the claim that the body was hidden inside the monster costume at the film lot.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction
Proposition 15 states that the president's body is inside the monster costume and the monster costume is inside the film lot, therefore the president's body is inside the film lot. This proposition seems to support Lang's testimony.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction
However, Evidence 5 describes the crime scene, stating that the body was lying face-up on top of the dirt, and yet there's no dirt on the front of the body. This evidence contradicts Testimony 4, which states that there's dirt inside the costume, implying that the body was hidden inside the costume and got dirty.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction
The spatial reasoning used here is that if the body was indeed hidden inside the monster costume, it would likely have gotten dirty, especially since the costume was supposedly used to hide the body in a dirty environment (as implied by the dirt inside the costume). However, the evidence shows that the body itself was not dirty on the front side, suggesting that it may not have been hidden inside the costume after all.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format
{"evidence": 5, "testimony": 4}

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction.

This proposition is relevant because it implies that Andrews, upon entering the room, would have seen the victim and the state of the room, including any objects like the vase.

Proposition 4 is applicable because it highlights the visibility of the crime scene to Andrews. **Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction. 
While Proposition 4 provides a framework for understanding the visibility and thus the potential for alteration of the crime scene, the core contradiction arises from the difference between Andrews' initial description of the scene and her later admission of altering it, which directly conflicts with the static representation of the scene in the crime scene photo.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format. 
Given the analysis, the contradiction lies between Evidence 9 (the crime scene photo showing the intact scene) and Testimony 5 (Andrews' admission to knocking over the vase, thus altering the scene). Therefore, the evidence and testimony that contradict each other are Evidence 9 and Testimony 5.

This directly contradicts the implication that Athena held the lighter in her left hand, as suggested by Aura's Statement and supported by Proposition 8.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction
The contradiction here arises from the spatial arrangement of fingerprints on the lighter and the hand with which it was held. The testimonies and evidences provide conflicting information about whether the lighter was held in the left or right hand, based on the fingerprints found on it.
